5.2  CONGESTION HEDGING MARKET 

In the Integrated Marketplace, the locational marginal prices assessed to load are generally 

higher than the locational marginal prices assessed to generators. The largest portion of this 

price difference is almost always attributed to congestion. This is an expected outcome and 

central to the design of nodal electricity markets. The difference between what SPP collects and 

pays out from virtuals, import/exports, loads and generators is often referred to as congestion 

rent. SPP remains revenue neutral in all Integrated Marketplace transactions and therefore must 
allocate the congestion rent back to the market participants. 104 The congestion hedging market 
is the mechanism used to allocate day-ahead congestion over-collections.105  

  MARKET DESIGN 

Market participants participate in the congestion hedging market by obtaining auction revenue 

rights and/or transmission congestion rights. Auction revenue rights begin as entitlements 

associated with long-term, firm transmission service reservations. These transmission service 

reservations are a revenue source for transmission owners and an expense for transmission 

customers. More specifically, transmission owners receive revenues from transmission customers 

for building and maintaining the transmission lines, and transmission customers pay the 

transmission owners for the use of the lines by way of the charges associated with transmission 
service reservations.106 

Auction revenue rights link the transmission service, which provides physical rights, to the 

Integrated Marketplace by converting these to financial rights. SPP verifies transmission service 

entitlements, which become candidate auction revenue rights. To obtain auction revenue rights, 

market participants nominate candidate auction revenue rights in the annual auction which 

awards revenue rights from June to May. If the nominations pass the allocation’s simultaneous 

feasibility test, the candidate auction revenue rights become auction revenue rights. The 

simultaneous feasibility test ensures that the market’s aggregate nomination of auction revenue 
rights does not violate thermal constraint limits under a single contingency.107 The test 

incorporates information from the network model, which aids SPP in aligning the supply of 

auction revenue rights with the capacity of the underlying transmission system. The 

simultaneous feasibility test aims to ensure the revenues generated from the congestion right 

auction will sufficiently fund the quantity of auction revenue rights nominated. If a candidate 

104 Schedule 1A fees are the exception. They are not redistributed through the market.  
105 With respect to day-ahead congestion rent only. 
106 These charges are assessed through transmission settlements and include various tariff schedules. 
107 SPP Open Access Transmission Tariff, Section 7.2.2, Simultaneous Feasibility. 

State of the Market 2024 

174 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

auction revenue right nomination fails the simultaneous feasibility test, this reduces the quantity 

of auction revenue rights successfully converted from candidate rights. 

Once market participants have successfully nominated their candidates into auction revenue 

rights, they must choose to either hold their auction revenue right or convert it into a 
transmission congestion right through a process known as self-conversion.108 If a market 

participant holds their auction revenue right, they will receive, or pay, a stream of unchanged 

cash flows over the life of the product. The size and direction of the cash flow depends on the 

market’s collective assessment of the congestion rent along the auction revenue right path as 

assessed by prices during the transmission congestion right auction. If a market participant 

believes that the auction prices will undervalue the congestion rent associated with their auction 

revenue right, the market participant will likely self-convert. When a market participant self-

converts, their auction revenue right becomes a transmission congestion right, which means 

their cash flow is subject to fluctuations in day-ahead market congestion rents.  

Financial-only109 market participants participate alongside traditional utilities in the transmission 

congestion right auctions to provide additional liquidity and price discovery. All participants 

compete on price for the residual network capacity. The auction software attempts to maximize 

auction revenue without violating the simultaneous feasibility test. This test helps align the 

supply of transmission congestion rights with the residual capacity of the underlying 

transmission system. If a transmission congestion right bid fails the simultaneous feasibility test, 

the quantity of transmission congestion rights successfully obtained will be reduced to the point 

where the bid no longer violates the test. Once a market participant obtains a transmission 

congestion right, they can hold it through to settlement, offer it for sale in a subsequent auction, 

or transact on the bulletin board outside of the auction cycle. The overwhelming majority of 

positions are held through to settlement. 

In addition to auction revenue rights, SPP may also award long-term transmission congestion 

rights. Much like auction revenue rights, these are awarded based on transmission ownership. 

However, long-term transmission congestion rights require the ownership of transmission with 

roll-over rights, and the ownership must span the entire TCR year. Long-term transmission 

congestion rights must also pass a simultaneous feasibility study, similar to auction revenue 

rights. Awarded long-term transmission congestion rights are automatically converted to 

transmission congestion rights for the entire year and settled in the same manner as 

conventional transmission congestion rights. These long-term congestion rights, once originally 

awarded, stay in effect until either the underlying transmission in relinquished or the owner of 

108 SPP Open Access Transmission Tariff, Section 7.5.1 (2) 
109 Financial-only market participants do not generate or serve load in the SPP footprint. 

State of the Market 2024 

175 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

the long-term transmission congestion rights removes the nomination. SPP limits the total 

amount of long-term transmission congestion rights awarded each auction to 50% of the 

available transmission capacity.

Payments to load-serving entities of $1.8 billion exceeded their day-ahead congestion costs of 

$1.3 billion in 2024. Additionally, real-time congestion costs aided load-serving entities by $33

not meet their day-ahead and real-time market congestions costs of $626 million. Overall, day-

ahead congestion costs were nearly $2.2 billion. This is a 24% increase from $1.7 billion in 2023, 

but still down from $2.7 billion in 2022.

176 

 
  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

  Bidding behaviors 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

As noted above, in aggregate, load-serving entities received more revenue from their 

congestion hedges than they paid in day-ahead and real-time congestion cost. However, on the 

individual participant level, some load-serving entities under-hedged while others over-hedged.  

Figure 5–21 shows, by load-serving market participant, the day-ahead congestion exposure 

along with the value of auction revenue rights and transmission congestion rights as well as the 

net overall position.  

Figure 5–21  Net congestion revenue by market participant 

The figure highlights that the majority of load-serving participants received positive net 

revenues, while a handful of participants held portfolios that did not cover their day-ahead 

congestion costs. For instance, the bottom five participants paid $181 million more in 

congestion costs than was offset by their auction revenue right and transmission congestion 

right positions. This is much more than the $79 million paid by the bottom five participants in 

the 2023 calendar year, but much less than the$261 million paid by the bottom five in 2022. 

The range of participant outcomes is influenced by three main factors: hedging need, individual 

participant bidding behavior, and the market’s collective bidding behavior.  

With respect to hedging needs, each participant experiences varying levels of congestion 

exposure mostly related to geographic location and type of physical interconnection. The 

various levels of congestion exposure lead to different hedging needs among market 

participants. 

State of the Market 2024 

177 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

The bidding behavior of the individual participant affects the auction revenue rights they receive 

through the allocation. Participants can, and do, employ numerous strategies with varying 

degrees of success.  

The bidding behavior of the other market participants, as a whole, affects the ability of each and 

every other market participant to obtain hedges through the auction revenue right allocation. 

More specifically, if a transaction is physically feasible with respect to transmission service, and 

by extension the day-ahead market, it does not necessarily mean the transaction will also be 

feasible in the auction revenue right allocation. The issue arises because the transmission 
system’s capacity, represented by transmission service requests,110 includes both prevailing flow 

and counter-flow transactions. However, participants often choose not to nominate their 

counter-flow candidate auction revenue rights in the allocation process, in part, because these 

positions tend to carry negative cash flows. These incentives motivate individual participants to 
abstain from counter-flow positions.111 By not nominating all candidate auction revenue rights, 

the capacity in the allocation will not match the capacity of the physical system. Because the 

basis of the auction revenue right is transmission service, if the two capacities do not align, a 

participant’s auction revenue right may not perfectly hedge their transmission service and their 

related day-ahead market activity.  

Differences between outages modeled in the auction processes and day-ahead market can also 

affect market participants’ ability to obtain hedges. Details on outage modeling are discussed 

below. 

  Transmission outage modeling 

When there are outages in the day-ahead market that were not in the transmission congestion 

rights auction, there is a reduction in day-ahead system capacity, which can cause underfunding. 

Figure 5–22 shows transmission outages by reported lead time.

Ideally, outages in the transmission congestion rights markets would be perfectly aligned with 

the day-ahead market. However, the MMU understands the challenges associated with 

accounting for outages in the transmission congestion rights market and recognizes that there 

can never be an exact match among the markets. Even so, improving how outages are handled 

and accounted for in the auction processes could help to reduce underfunding. The MMU has 

made recommendations for SPP to improve how outages are accounted for in the transmission 
congestion right auction process to improve the congestion hedging market results.114

rights could be cause for concern. The market monitor continues to encourage SPP to review

INTRA-AUCTION SALES AND BULLETIN BOARD 
TRANSACTIONS 

Intra-auction sales refer to the sale of a previously acquired transmission congestion right 

position in a subsequent auction. Bulletin board transactions refer to trades where a market 

participant buys or sells a position outside of the auction cycle through the SPP bulletin board. 

Overall, both inter-auction sales and bulletin board transactions remain low. 

Figure 5–29 shows the transaction volume by type as a percentage of all transmission 

congestion right purchase volume.  

State of the Market 2024 

184 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

Figure 5–29  Intra-auction sales and bulletin board transactions 

No bulletin board transactions cleared in 2024, just like 2018 through 2021. A small number 

cleared in 2022 and 2023. In both those years the bulletin board transactions were less than 

0.05% of all congestion right transactions. Intra-auction sales remained low at 8% of the total 

transmission congestion right volume. However, this is an increase from the 2022 and 2023 

levels of 6%. 

Outside of their relationship to the auction cycle, these transactions also differ from each other 

in another material way. Bulletin board transactions are similar to the secondary equity market, 

where a share of stock is offered for sale and that same share is later purchased by another 

market participant. As such, the bulletin board transactions do not affect total supply; they only 

affect ownership of the existing supply.  

However, intra-auction sales can affect supply in addition to ownership. When market 

participants offer their prevailing flow positions for sale intra-auction, the capacity of those 

positions once used is available to the market again. But the newly available system capacity can 

be taken up by any path, not just the path sold intra-auction. Furthermore, to sell counter-flow 

positions intra-auction, unclaimed prevailing flow capacity must exist for the transaction to 
clear.118 This is because counter-flow intra-auction sales reduce the total capacity available. The 

counter-flow now offered for sale previously facilitated other prevailing flow positions. If the 

counter-flow sale were to clear without considering supply, the prevailing flow once facilitated 

by this counter-flow would no longer be feasible. So, in order for these existing prevailing flow 

transactions to remain feasible, additional prevailing flow capacity must exist. Practically, the 

additional prevailing flow capacity required plays the same role once played by the counter-flow 

118 The additional capacity could also be provided by another counter-flow intra-auction bid.  

State of the Market 2024 

185 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

being offered for sale. These circumstances likely also incentivize market participant abstentions 

from counter-flow. Generally, if a market participant holds a counter-flow position, it could be 

very difficult to sell the position intra-auction, which is the main source of intra-marketplace 
liquidity.119  

ISSUES, PROGRESS, AND NEXT STEPS  

The following four areas highlight where continued progress could bring about improved 

market outcomes, risk reduction, and efficiency gains.  

1.  Obtaining long-term congestion rights and auction revenue rights 

Market participants currently experience varying levels of success in obtaining long-term 

congestion rights, as well as auction revenue rights and by extension self-converted 

transmission congestion rights. Improving the process to better align transmission 

service with long-term congestion right and auction revenue right entitlements would 

likely improve the outcomes materially. 

In July 2024 FERC approved SPP’s HITT M1 design to improve congestion hedging.120 121  

There were five changes brought through the revision: 

•  Modify the long-term congestion right (LTCR) round 2 allocation’s simultaneous 
feasibility test (SFT) from a single two-part iteration allocation (one for load 
serving entities and one for non-load serving entities) to a five two-part iteration 
process.  

•  Modify the nomination capacity calculation for round 1 of the annual auction 

revenue right (ARR) allocation process.  

•  Modify the annual auction revenue right round 1 simultaneous feasibility test 

(SFT) from a single iteration allocation to a five iteration process.  

•  Modify the distribution of excess auction revenues with a phased in approach 

(50% current method and 50% new method year 1 and then 100% new method 
going forward) using the nomination cap minus awards methodology.  

•  Update generator modeling to better align with transmission service studied. 

The MMU filed in support of these changes and believes they will help increase 

participants’ abilities to obtain auction revenue rights and by extension congestion 

hedges. The MMU will continue to monitor the effectiveness of the changes as the 

congestion hedging markets proceed. The MMU still believes more equity can be 

119 Intra-market refers to inside the SPP Integrated Marketplace. 
120 Revision Request RR591 HITT M1 Congestion Hedging Improvements 
121 ER24-1775 

State of the Market 2024 

186 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Congestion and transmission congestion rights 

provided in the LTCR and ARR allocation process. However, this would cause a 

redistribution of congestion hedging rights, a process that members have not been able 

to agree upon. 

2.  Credit policy 

The Credit Practices Working Group developed and implemented the first phase of credit 

policy enhancements in 2022. These enhancements are a material improvement. The 

second phase focused on incorporating forward-looking known information into the 

financial security calculations. SPP staff reviewed options for a forward-looking approach 

in 2021, including a mark-to-auction process. However, no changes were implemented. 

In July 2022, FERC issued an order to show cause122 to four ISO/RTOs as to why their 

tariffs were just and reasonable in absence of either of (1) mark-to-auction mechanisms 

for the calculation of financial transmission right (FTR) market participants’ collateral 

requirements and/or (2) volumetric minimum collateral requirements for FTR market 

participants. SPP therefore was directed to show cause as to why its tariff is just and 

reasonable without mark-to-auction mechanisms.  

SPP originally filed comments in support of the current tariff and the MMU filed in 
support of mark-to-auction. 123 FERC responded that SPP had not shown satisfactory 

evidence that the tariff was just and reasonable and asked that SPP show cause as to why 
the tariff was just and reasonable. 124 In November 2023, SPP responded with a request 

for abeyance of FERC’s 206. SPP stated it had a proposed mark-to-auction design they 

would like to bring forward through the stakeholder process and ultimately to FERC in 
June 2023. The MMU filed in support of the abeyance. 125 SPP filed Tariff language with 
FERC outlining their mark-to-auction proposal.126 The MMU ultimately filed in protest 

due to the fact that the proposal does not allow all TCR collateral holds to adjust 
throughout the year as the perceived financial risks for the underlying TCRs change. 127 

The filing is awaiting FERC’s review and finding.  

3.  Secondary intra-market liquidity 

Intra-auction sales continue to represent modest transaction volume. The limited 

liquidity associated with these products is not unique to the SPP markets; however,

Competitive assessment 

6  COMPETITIVE ASSESSMENT 

Chapter 6 of this report provides a competitive assessment of the SPP market. Key points from 

this chapter include: 

•  Structural and behavioral metrics indicate that the SPP markets have been competitive 

over the last several years. The market share, Herfindahl-Hirschman Index (HHI), and 

pivotal supplier analyses all indicate minimal to moderate potential structural market 

power in SPP markets outside of frequently constrained areas. 

• 

In 14% of the hours in 2024, the market share of the largest on-line supplier in terms of 

real time energy output exceeded 20%, a declining trend observed since the June 2018 

formation of Evergy, Inc. as SPP’s largest market participant. 

•  The HHI market concentration analysis shows that 2% of hours were considered 

moderately concentrated in 2024 showing a decline from the 3% level in 2023. The SPP 

market remained mostly unconcentrated—with 90% of all intervals since 2019 

considered unconcentrated. No intervals were considered highly concentrated in 2024, 

and the percentage of intervals considered unconcentrated (98%) was at its highest level 

since 2017. 

•  The results of the pivotal supplier analyses indicate that the percent of hours with a 

pivotal supplier is the highest in two zones—New Mexico/West Texas and 

Iowa/Dakotas/Montana—with nearly all intervals possessing pivotal suppliers regardless 

of demand.  

•  Off-peak and on-peak annual average markups continued to be in the negative territory 

in 2024, off-peak period being more pronounced. Wind resources, however, continue to 

offer at exceptionally low markup levels. 

•  Positive markups were observed for coal at slightly decreasing levels relative to the 

positive markups seen in 2023 but much lower than 2022. Negative on-peak and off-

peak markups were observed in almost every month for natural gas resources in 2024, 

except for January, with a clear correlation to the prevailing price of gas within the SPP 

footprint. All months saw negative markups for wind resources, with markup levels 

State of the Market 2024 

189 

 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Competitive assessment 

continuing to stay low throughout the years. The average markups for wind resources 

were −$37.99 in 2024 −$41.50 in 2023 and −$38.75 in 2022, respectively. 

• 

Incremental energy offer mitigation in 2024 stayed at roughly the same in frequency in 

both the day-ahead and the real-time market; meaning, energy offer mitigation 

continues to remain very rare, at 0.04% and 0.22% of resource hours in the day-ahead 

and real-time markets, respectively. However, day-ahead no-load mitigation sharply 

increased because some resources with high no-load offers relative to their mitigated 

offer hit a binding constraint. 

•  Behavioral metrics suggest that attempts to actually exercise market power by 

manipulating the price (economic withholding) or quantity (physical withholding) of 

generation are rare. The output gap, an inference of economically withheld generation, 

was slightly higher in 2024. Overall, it still remains at very low levels, averaging less than 

0.2% in all months.  

The SPP Integrated Marketplace provides sufficient market incentives to produce competitive 

market outcomes in regions and periods lacking local market power. The MMU’s competitive 

assessment provides evidence that market outcomes were workably competitive in 2024, 

requiring only infrequent mitigation of local market power. 

The market power analysis in this report considers both structural and behavioral aspects of 

market power concerns. Structural aspects are examined with techniques such as market share 

analysis, market-wide concentration indices, and pivotal supplier analysis. These structural 

indicators illuminate the potential for market power without regard to the actual exercise of 

market power. Behavioral analyses, on the other hand, look for the exercise of market power by 

assessing the actual offer or bid conduct of market participants, and the impact of that conduct 

on market prices. These analyses examined offer price markup, economic withholding, the 

frequency of automated mitigation, uneconomic production, and physical withholding. 

This chapter evaluates the SPP market’s competitive environment by establishing the level of 

structural market power and then examining market prices for indications of the exercise of that 

power. Structural market power is assessed both at the SPP footprint level through supplier 

concentration indices and market share levels, and at the local (transmission-constrained) level 

through pivotal supplier analysis. In the SPP markets, mitigation of economic withholding is 

accomplished ex-ante through automatic market power mitigation processes that limit the 

ability of generators with market power to raise prices above competitive levels. The mitigation 

program is monitored and evaluated to ensure it is efficient and effective. Accordingly, the 

State of the Market 2024 

190 

 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Competitive assessment 

following subsections examine the significance of market power and the effectiveness of local 

market power mitigation in the SPP markets. 

6.1  STRUCTURAL ASPECTS OF MARKET POWER 

Three core metrics of structural market power are the market share analysis, the Herfindahl-

Hirschman Index (HHI), and pivotal supplier analysis. The first two of these indicators measure 

market-wide concentration, ignoring local constraints. Pivotal supplier analysis, on the other 

hand, accounts for the dynamics of power markets and considers changing demand conditions 

and locational transmission constraints in assessing potential market power. 

Figure 6–1 

 displays the market share of the largest on-line supplier in terms of energy 

dispatch in the real-time market by hour for 2024.  

Figure 6–1  Market share of largest supplier 

The market share ranged from 9% to 25%, exceeding the 20% threshold128 in 1,228 hours (14%) 

for the year. In 2023, market shares ranged from 11% to 26%, with market shares exceeding the 

20% threshold in 29% of all intervals. This continues a trend of decreasing concentration after an 

acute rise in most concentration metrics following the formation of Evergy, Inc. in 2018. Evergy 

remains the largest on-line supplier in over 97% of intervals. Note that although a mere increase 

in market share does not itself pose a threat to the structural competitiveness of the SPP market, 

128 The 20% threshold is a historically accepted standard for identifying structural market power. Note, 
however, that neither market share nor the HHI metric alone would be sufficient for the assessment of 
market power particularly in today’s spot electricity markets where load pockets formed by transmission 
congestion may lead to market power with much smaller market shares and/or HHI values.

other relevant market data including pivotal supplier hours and local market power mitigation 

must also be closely evaluated for competitive assessment (see below).

As shown above, the market remained mostly unconcentrated from the addition of the 
Integrated System to the date of the Great Plains/Westar merger that created Evergy.131 Even 

though moderately concentrated hours increased following the creation of Evergy, only 9, 3, and 

2% of hours fell into this category in 2022, 2023, and 2024, respectively. In contrast, in 2014, just 

over 50% of hours were considered moderately concentrated, and in 2015, prior to the addition 

of the Integrated System, nearly 40% of hours were moderately concentrated. 

While moderately concentrated hours increased following the creation of Evergy, it is not 

necessarily a cause for alarm, as they still remain below 2015 levels, prior to the Integrated 

System joining the market. Later metrics in this report will more closely examine specific market 
behavior by market participants in terms of the exercise of local market power.132 

The MMU’s market share analysis and HHI metrics both indicate a moderate potential for 

general structural market power in SPP markets outside of frequently constrained areas. 

Structural market power is also assessed at a more localized level and in the context of 

locational transmission constraints by reevaluating frequently constrained areas periodically and 

(re)defining them, accordingly, as discussed in Section 5.1.7. 

Pivotal supplier analysis considers the dynamic nature of the power market, particularly variable 

demand conditions, and evaluates the potential for market power in the presence of pivotal 

suppliers. A supplier is pivotal when market demand cannot be met without some or all its 

generation. There may be one or more pivotal suppliers in a particular market defined by 

transmission constraints and load conditions, and a supplier’s pivotal status may vary between 

time periods irrespective of its size. 

The following metric identifies the frequency with which at least one supplier was pivotal in the 
five different reserve zones133 (regions) of the SPP footprint in 2024.134 The frequency with which 

a supplier is pivotal is an indication of their potential to raise prices above competitive levels. 

While the mere size of a supplier does not itself render the supplier pivotal, suppliers which are 

131 Note that the HHI analysis is performed at the market participant level. There may be asset owners 
under market participants on a contractual basis, where bidding control is not under the purview of the 
market participant.

mapped to one of these reserve zones. To define a load zone to match with a resource zone, each load 
settlement location was mapped to a reserve zone to approximate demand within a particular zone. 
Additionally, import limits are approximated by the average of the reserve zone limits for the times they 
were activated in 2023.

frequently pivotal in high-demand intervals have a greater ability to exercise market power. For

2024. 

6.2  BEHAVIORAL ASPECTS OF MARKET POWER 

  OFFER PRICE MARKUP 

In a competitive market, prices should reflect the short-run marginal cost of producing the 

marginal unit. In SPP’s Integrated Marketplace, market participants submit hourly mitigated 

energy offer curves that represent their short-run marginal cost of energy. Market participants 

also submit their market-based offers, which may differ from their mitigated offers. To assess 

market performance, a comparison is made between the market offer and the mitigated offer 

for the marginal resources for each real-time market interval.  

State of the Market 2024 

195 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Competitive assessment 

Figure 6–6 provides the average marginal resource offer price markups135 by month for on-peak 

and off-peak periods. Similar to 2023, the MMU observed negative markups on average in 2024. 

2022 experienced positive average markups for the summer peaking months due to high natural 

gas prices and coal shortage issues. This trend did not continue in 2023, as the coal shortage 

situation improved and as natural gas prices decreased.  

Figure 6–6  Average offer price markup of marginal resource, monthly 

In 2024, average monthly markups ranged from −$25.78 /MWh to −$1.26/MWh for off-peak 

periods and from −$21.08/MWh to $2.40/MWh for on-peak periods. The lowest markups 

occurred in spring off-peak and on-peak hours when wind generation was generally the highest. 

We also observed lower markups in off-peak hours than in on-peak hours in 2024.

Although a lower offer price markup level in itself would indicate competitive pressure on 

suppliers in the SPP market, this continuous and intensifying downward trend may raise 

questions about the continued commercial viability of generating units, as well as inappropriate 

behavior.136 Despite this trend, SPP continues to add new generation (mostly wind). More 

renewable generation is anticipated to be added over the next few years, which is expected to 

outweigh planned retirements. As such the trend in negative offer-price markups will likely 

continue, which may pressure the economic viability of older generating resources.

  MITIGATION PERFORMANCE AND FREQUENCY 

SPP resources’ incremental energy, start-up, no-load, and operating reserve offers are subject to 

mitigation for economic withholding when the following three circumstances occur 

simultaneously in a market solution: 

1)  The resource has local market power; 

2)  The offer has failed the conduct test. Resources submit two offers for each product: a 

mitigated offer representing the competitive baseline costs that must adhere to the 

mitigated offer development guidelines139 and a second offer generally referred to as a 

market offer, which often includes risk-based and strategy-based adjustments. An offer is 

considered for mitigation when the market offer exceeds the mitigated offer by more 

than the allowed threshold; and 

3)  The resource either: 

a)  Is manually committed by SPP for capacity, transmission constraint, or voltage 

support; or by a local transmission operator for local transmission problems or 

voltage support; or  

b)  The application of mitigation impacts market prices or make-whole payments by 

more than the allowed $25/MWh threshold. 

In 2024, day-ahead mitigation frequency rose for energy and no-load, but dropped for 

operating reserves. Overall, levels remain very low at less than 0.75% across energy, no-load, 

138 Tariff rules only allow for submitting monotonically non-decreasing offer curves by market participants, 
and this may result in market offers by natural gas units below their mitigated offers during periods with 
negative prevailing gas prices. Additionally, real-time market offers may be below mitigated offers when 
natural gas units’ mitigated offers are indexed to hub prices when in fact the cost of gas received could be 
below that hub price. Some SPP market participants have experienced negative natural gas prices in the 
past, but it remains a rare occurrence.

  OUTPUT GAP (MEASURE FOR POTENTIAL ECONOMIC 

WITHHOLDING) 

Economic withholding is defined as submitting a resource offer that is artificially high, such that 

either the resource will not be scheduled or dispatched, or—if scheduled or dispatched—the 

offer will set a higher than competitive market clearing price. Accordingly, the output gap metric 

aims to measure the economic (or competitive) amount of output withheld from the market 

through the submission of offers in excess of competitive levels. The output gap is the amount 

of generation not produced as a result of offers exceeding the mitigated offer above an 

appropriate conduct threshold. The conduct threshold is employed to compensate for any 

inaccuracies or uncertainties in estimating the cost, similar to the one used in economic 

withholding mitigation. In this report, the output gap is calculated as the difference between a 

resource’s economic level of output at the prevailing market clearing price and the greater of 

actual offered MWs and actual amount of production. The economic level of output is produced 
by a generator between its minimum and maximum economic capacity.140  

The MMU employs a 17.5% conduct threshold for the frequently constrained areas and a 25% 

conduct threshold for the rest of the footprint to reflect the actual thresholds used in the 
clearing process’s automatic economic withholding mitigation.141 In order to account for the 

discrepancy between a resource’s offered capacity and the dispatched amount (due to possible 

limitations in real-time market conditions such as transmission constraints, operator actions or 

ramp limitations, and virtual participants), an upward adjustment is made by taking the greater 

of the day-ahead scheduled or the real-time dispatched amount to reflect the actual amount of 

production.  

Note that certain market conditions such as congestion (supplier location), supplier size, or high 

demand can create the potential for exercising market power and can facilitate economic 

withholding behavior. For this reason, the output gap is calculated as percentages of total 

economic output withheld compared to total reference capacity for the SPP footprint. In 

addition, the output gap is calculated for the largest three suppliers (market participant 

portfolios) in each frequently constrained area, and if so designated, comparing levels to those 

140 The MMU calculates this metric by including all resources’ total (reference level) capacity when 
calculating output gap percentages. 
141 The Joplin frequently constrained areas were inactivated on July 10, 2024, and the Oklahoma City, Tulsa, 
and Williston are staying activated in 2024 FCA analysis. Therefore, four frequently constrained areas were 
shown in this 2024 report. 

State of the Market 2024 

202 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Competitive assessment 

of the remaining suppliers. Similar to last year’s report, annual calculations were run for all days 

and evaluated at varying levels of demand as a potential market condition that can affect 

withholding outcomes. 

Figure 6–12 below shows the monthly level of the output gap across the SPP footprint from 

2022 to 2024.  

Figure 6–12  Output gap, monthly 

Compared to the previous year, 2024’s output gap was around 0.06 percentage points higher. 

Overall, the output gap remains at very low levels, averaging less than 0.3% in all months and 

reflects a high level of participation in the market overall. While Figure 6–13 displays the output 

gap calculated by demand level and participant size for the entire SPP market footprint, Figure 
6–14 shows the output gap for the four frequently constrained areas for 2024.142 There were no 

observed output gaps in the Williston frequently constrained areas for any demand and supply 

levels in 2024.

and Williston are staying activated in 2024 FCA analysis. Therefore, four frequently constrained areas were 
shown in this 2024 report.

Though output gaps continue to remain at low levels within the SPP market footprint, higher 

output gap levels were generally observed for the top three largest suppliers at all demand 

periods in 2024. The results indicate a very low level of economic output withheld in the SPP 

footprint. There was a similar pattern observed in the frequently constrained areas with a little 

higher level of output gap for the three top suppliers. These outcomes are generally consistent 

with expectations of competitive market conduct. In general, more output is expected to be 

withheld at higher demand levels or by larger suppliers. However, at times, output may also be 

withheld in low load periods as prices are often negative during the lowest 20% of load hours.

Competitive assessment 

  UNOFFERED GENERATION CAPACITY (MEASURE FOR 

POTENTIAL PHYSICAL WITHHOLDING)  

As part of the competitive assessment, the MMU reviewed potential physical withholding 

behaviors by generators throughout 2021 through 2024. Physical withholding refers to conduct 

where a supplier derates a resource or otherwise does not offer it into the market. Physical 

withholding may include: intentionally not following dispatch instructions, declaring false 

derates or outages, refusing to provide offers, or providing inaccurate resource parameters such 

as capability limitations. Any economic generation capacity that is not made available to the 
market through a derate, outage, or by other means is considered for this analysis.143,144 

Total economic capacity that was derated from respective reference levels was classified by 

reason and duration. Derates can be reflected as planned or forced outages submitted through 
SPP’s outage scheduling system or any undesignated unoffered capacity.145 Any derates from 

reference levels are considered in this analysis. 

Derates were classified as either short-term or long-term. Those of less than seven days’ 

duration were classified as short-term and the rest as long-term. This is because the economic 

capacity that was not offered short-term has more potential for physical withholding relative to 

long-term derates as it would be less costly—because of loss of sales—for a supplier to withhold 

capacity for a short duration of time.  

As in the case for economic withholding, the potential for physical withholding is also affected 

by various market conditions at the time offers are made including location (congestion), 

supplier size, or demand levels. Larger suppliers would be in a more advantageous position to 

exercise market power. During tight market conditions, suppliers have more incentive and 

opportunity to physically withhold capacity for strategic reasons. In addition, scheduling 

maintenance outages in high demand periods may indicate a strategic behavior to create 

artificial shortages.  

The MMU assessed derated and unoffered economic capacity both in the day-ahead and real-

time markets. Similar to the output gap analysis, the MMU modeled commitments based on 
day-ahead market outcomes for all units.146 The unoffered capacity is calculated as the 

143 This analysis, in part, draws on “Assessment of the Market Monitoring Metrics for the SPP Energy 
Imbalance Service (EIS) Market,” Potomac Economics, December 2010 and “2016 State of the Market 
Report for the New York ISO Markets,” Potomac Economics, May 2017. 
144 Economic offered capacity is determined in a similar way as in the output gap analysis in Section 6.2.3 
by comparing resource’s (cost-based) mitigated offer to the prevailing locational price.  
145 The planned maintenance outages by nuclear generation and unoffered capacity by hydro, wind, and 
solar is excluded in this analysis. 
146 Due to the new fast-start logic implemented in 2022.

difference between the unit’s economic capacity147 and its offered maximum economic capacity 

operating limit during intervals when the unit was deemed economic (i.e., covering its costs 

given the clearing price).

levels by month, frequently constrained area,148 and by supplier (participant) size against varying 
load levels for the SPP footprint.149

7  RECOMMENDATIONS 

Recommendations 

One of the core functions of a market monitor is “to advise the Commission, the RTO or ISO, 
and other interested entities of its views regarding any needed rule and tariff changes.”153 The 

MMU accomplishes this responsibility through many forums, including but not limited to active 

participation in the SPP stakeholder process, commenting on FERC notices of proposed 

rulemakings, submitting comments to FERC on SPP filings, and making recommendations in the 

annual state of the market report.  

This section outlines the MMU recommendations to SPP and stakeholders to address our 

concerns with current market design, rules, and processes, highlighting both updates made to 

prior recommendations along with new recommendations based on 2024 market outcomes. The 

MMU has identified four new recommendations for 2024.  

7.1  NEW RECOMMENDATIONS 

2024.1  MARKET-BASED INCENTIVE TO ENSURE DAILY 

AVAILABILITY OF ADEQUATE ACCREDITED CAPACITY TO 
MEET DAILY LOAD 

From 2021 to 2024, the SPP region has experienced four major winter weather events. During 

those events, SPP spent a total of 335 hours in conservative operations, 44 hours in an Energy 

Emergency Alert (EEA) level 1, 43 hours in EEA level 2, and 9 hours in EEA level 3.  

SPP has made a number of tariff revisions since 2021 to address reliability, including establishing 

a winter season resource adequacy requirement and a separate winter season planning reserve 

margin, which will be implemented for the 2026 winter season. SPP has also filed revisions to 

attachment AA of the SPP Open Access Transmission Tariff (Tariff) with the Federal Energy 

Regulatory Commission (FERC) to better align resource accreditation with past performance. In 

addition to tariff revisions, the North American Electric Reliability Corporation (NERC) has made 

updates to their reliability requirements. In response to the new NERC standards and the more 

frequent severe weather events, resource owners have made material improvements to try to 

ensure increased availability and reliability of their resources. 

Since 2021, there has been a marginal increase in overall resource performance, particularly 

during severe weather events, but the overall reliability in the region remains challenged due to 

load growth. Additionally, there is no immediate incentive, through the resource adequacy 

153 As defined by FERC in Order No. 719. 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Recommendations 

construct, for a resource to be available to serve load on any given day. Therefore, to ensure 

reliability and an efficient, competitive market, the MMU recommends the development of a 

market incentive for resources to meet regional load obligations on a daily basis. This incentive 

should focus on accredited resources but be structured to benefit any resource when there is a 

shortage of available accredited resources. The MMU prefers a market-based solution to capture 

the efficiency gains from a competitive market. Alternatively, a non-market solution could be 

used to incentivize capacity to be available to serve load obligations on a daily basis. A non-

market solution would need to mirror the accreditation process with an ex-post review of a load 

responsible entity’s (LRE) actual load compared to pre-season forecast, and the availability of 

accredited resources across the season with emphasis on the tightest periods. 

2024.2  SUFFICIENCY VALUATION CURVE TRANSPARENCY  

A critical piece of the supply adequacy process is an effective incentive structure to ensure LREs 

meet their individual Resource Adequacy Requirements (RARs). The MMU, in collaboration with 

SPP and stakeholders, designed the Sufficiency Valuation Curve (SVC) as an alternative to the 

standard deficiency payment when a deficiency can be attributed to a sudden and significant 

change in the Planning Reserve Margin (PRM). While the standard deficiency payment charges a 

multiple of the Cost of New Entry (CONE) to incentivize new entry, the SVC instead estimates the 

value of excess accredited capacity in the region based on CONE, net CONE, and the amount of 

total accredited capacity available to the region relative to the region’s non-coincident peak 

load and PRM. Payments collected via the SVC are distributed to excess accredited capacity as 

compensation for standing in for deficient LREs. 

The MMU is concerned that the effectiveness of the SVC as an incentive for resource adequacy 

is at risk due to a lack of transparency and a need for process improvements. As such, we first 

recommend that SPP recalculate the value of net CONE annually. In addition, this value and the 

clearing price of the SVC should be published publicly once available to provide transparent and 

symmetric information to all LREs and market participants in order to allow them to make more 

informed decisions related to capacity in the future. 

2024.3  IDENTIFICATION OF AFFILIATES IN THE MARKET  

The lack of transparency of the affiliates in the SPP markets leads to less efficient market 

surveillance and challenges in detecting potential gaming and market manipulation. The MMU 

recommends SPP implement a design similar to the current WEIS Market design in which a 

market participant must identify all of its affiliates within the market at the time of registration. 

Existing participants must timely update their registration to reflect current affiliations. The MMU 

recommends the following definition for affiliate.  

State of the Market 2024 

211 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Recommendations 

Affiliate: With respect to a corporation, partnership or other entity, each such other corporation, 

partnership or other entity that directly or indirectly, through one or more intermediaries, controls, 

is controlled by, or is under common control with, such corporation, partnership or other entity as 

defined under 18 CFR 35.36(a)(9). 

2024.4 COST OF NEW ENTRY (CONE) UPDATE 

SPP's gross Cost of New Entry (CONE) study assumptions and the associated value, currently set 

at $85.61/kW-year, are outdated and require reevaluation. CONE is used to indicate the current, 

annualized, capital cost of a new hypothetical resource and estimate the breakeven revenue 

required for investment. In SPP's construct, the CONE value is used to determine the payment 

that a deficient load responsible entity is charged if they lack sufficient accredited capacity to 

cover their forecasted peak load and planning reserve margin. The existing value is based on 

assumptions from the Energy Information Agency's 2013 report, which became effective in 2018 

with the approval of SPP's Tariff Attachment AA by FERC in Docket ER18-1268-000. No 

adjustments have been made since inception to either the CONE values or the study 

assumptions for the process. 

Compared to other RTOs and ISOs, SPP's gross CONE value stands as an outlier, with a 

significantly lower value than other markets, and a comprehensive update incorporating modern 

assumptions is anticipated to result in a sizable increase. The current resource adequacy 

workplan lacks a scheduled reevaluation of the CONE value. SPP has indicated plans to initiate 

stakeholder discussions in time for a CONE update in 2028, which provides sufficient time to 

represent an opportunity for revamping the study process.  

To align with best practices and ensure market efficiency, SPP should incorporate into its 

resource advisory workplan a plan to reevaluate its CONE value and processes (such as selection 

of the reference technology type, frequency of CONE update, inflation assumptions, cost of 

capital percentage, transparency rules, etc.) and ensure that the CONE value is as accurate as 

practicable going forward. 

7.2  PREVIOUS RECOMMENDATIONS 

The MMU has provided recommendations to improve market design in each of our previous 

annual state of the market reports since the launch of the Integrated Marketplace in 2014. While 

some of these recommendations have been addressed, there remain many outstanding 

recommendations, with several being noted as high priority issues. A high-level summary of 

each of these recommendations, including recent updates and current status are outlined below. 

State of the Market 2024 

212 

 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Recommendations 

Additional information for these recommendations can be found in the corresponding annual 

state of the market report. 

2023.1  ENHANCE UNCERTAINTY PRODUCT DESIGN TO ENSURE 

PROCUREMENT OF ADEQUATE RAMPABLE CAPACITY   

In its 2023 annual report, the MMU included a recommendation to enhance the uncertainty 
product design to address deficiencies in the implemented product.154 There were two issues 

noted by the MMU: backing out other reserve product requirements from the uncertainty 

product’s requirement and how soon-to-start resources are treated in the optimization. The 

MMU recommended that SPP look critically at the practice of reducing the uncertainty 

requirement with other reserve products’ requirements as well as reviewing the use of soon-to-

start resources in meeting the requirement for uncertainty. 

In October 2024, SPP implemented an enhancement to the uncertainty product design to back 

out only the the ramp capability-up product’s requirement from the flexibility requirement in 

alignment with one part of the MMU recommendaton. This has led to some improvement in the 
clearing for the uncertainty product. 155 SPP has noted it will review the second part of the MMU 

recommendation, i.e. the treatment of soon-to-start resources, after having time to assess the 

results of the October 2024 enhancement. The MMU considers this a medium priority issue. 

2023.2  ENSURE PLANNING, MARKETS, AND OPERATIONAL 

PROCESSES APPROPRIATELY CONSIDER INTEGRATION OF 
LARGE LOADS.  

The number of large loads, such as large data centers and crypto mines, continue to rise in the 
SPP footprint, with many of them registering as demand response resources.156 Owing to their 

size and location, these loads have the potential to cause, and have caused, high prices and 

significant levels of congestion in both the day-ahead and real-time markets. Alignment of 

planning, markets, and operational processes to accommodate the integration of these large 

loads is necessary to ensure appropriate price signals in the market and address unsolved 

congestion impacts on the system. Furthermore, alignment also helps prevent operational issues 

that ultimately impact the market and other market participants. The MMU made 

recommendations in its 2023 report for the alignment of planning, markets, and operational 

154 2023 Annual State of the Market report, Chapter 7 Recommendations, 2023.1 Enhance uncertainty 
product design to ensure procurement of adequate rampable capacity

process to appropriately consider the integration of large loads.157 SPP has multiple ongoing 

initiatives to address the challenges with the addition and integration of large loads on the

issue. 

2022.1  CONSIDER LIMITATIONS ON VIRTUAL TRADING DURING 

EMERGENCY CONDITIONS 

In 2022, the MMU published a paper examining the impact of virtual trading during the 
February 2021 winter weather event.158 Based on the results of the analysis performed, the MMU 
submitted an initiative159 on the SPP Roadmap seeking to study the effectiveness of virtual 

transactions during the winter weather event and identify any potential lessons learned or 

recommendations going forward. The MMU also included this as a recommendation in its 2022 
annual report.160 This initiative was removed from the SPP Roadmap and added to Market 

Working Group’s list of action items to consider. The MMU continues to recommend that SPP 

and stakeholders consider the recommendations made in the MMU report related to the 

behavior of those submitting virtual transactions and, at a minimum, monitor metrics on the 

efficacy of virtual transactions as part of routine market analysis. The MMU considers this a low 

priority issue. 

2022.2  ADDRESS LIMITATIONS WITH THE RAMP CAPABILITY 

PRODUCT 

In fall 2022, the MMU performed a review of the ramp capability product’s effectiveness and 
documented the results in its quarterly report.161 In its 2022 annual report, the MMU 

recommended that SPP and stakeholders evaluate options to address issues with the ramp 

capability product, including issues with the demand curve values and ramp being stranded 
behind congested constraints.162  

SPP submitted an initiative on the SPP Roadmap in November 2022 to work with the MMU to 

analyze the recommendations made in the annual report. SPP subsequently closed the initiative,

and the recommendations made in the 2022 annual report are addressed. The MMU continues 

to recommend that SPP staff evaluate the effectiveness of the demand curves when assessing 

solutions to the stranded ramping issue. The MMU considers this a medium priority issue. 

2022.3  IMPROVE SITUATIONAL AWARENESS OF TRANSMISSION 
UPGRADES AND IMPROVE PROCESS TO REASSIGN 
PROJECTS 

Delays in transmission upgrades can significantly contribute to congestion. This can have 

significant ramifications for market outcomes, and costs paid by ratepayers. Moreover, resource 

decisions, including generator interconnection, are made based on expectations and 

information on the status of transmission upgrades.  

In the 2022 Annual Report, the MMU noted a significant number of transmission projects and 

upgrades that were behind expected relevant deadlines and made recommendations regarding 

the lack of transparency into the actual expected in-service dates and delays and also 

recommended that SPP improve tariff clarity to assist in determining if transmission projects 
should be reassigned.163 Due to work completed since the 2022 report, the MMU considers the 

portion of the recommendation regarding improved situational awareness of transmission 

upgrades to have been addressed through changes in regular in-service date delay reporting 
and processes outlined in changes to BP7060 from SPP RR 574.164 However, the portion of this 

recommendation regarding improvements to the process of reassigning projects remains 

outstanding. The MMU considers this a medium priority issue. 

2021.1  EXPAND MULTI-CONFIGURATION COMBINED CYCLE 

RESOURCE MODEL TO INCLUDE ADDITIONAL RESOURCE 
TYPES 

In the 2021 annual report,165 the MMU recommended SPP expand the multi-configuration 

combined cycle resource model or create a new multi-configuration model to include additional 

resource types that have multiple operating modes, or configurations. This recommendation

optimizing the schedule of configurations, improving participant abilities to offer different 

parameters for each configuration, and providing real-time operational awareness. 

Additional information, including further potential applications of the multi-configuration 

combined cycle resource model, is documented in the 2021 report. The MMU considers this a 

high priority issue. 

2020.1  UPDATE MARKET AND OUTAGE REQUIREMENTS TO 

IMPROVE FUNDING FOR TRANSMISSION CONGESTION 
RIGHTS 

The MMU made recommendations in the 2020 annual report to update outage requirements 

and develop market rules and market incentives associated with outages in order to better align 

the network models used by the transmission congestion rights auctions and the day-ahead 
market.166 This recommendation was a result of the MMU observation of a continued downward 

trend in the overall funding of transmission congestion rights from day-ahead market 

congestion rents.  

This recommendation is an initiative167 on the SPP Roadmap ranked as a high priority. In January 

2024, the Operating Reliability Working Group (ORWG) approved outage coordination 

methodology language changes requiring outages on transmission lines that are 100kV and 

above and five days or longer in duration be submitted to SPP 45 days ahead of the calendar 

month. The MMU noted in its 2024 annual report, that after that change was implemented, the 

MMU would monitor the impact of the change on TCR underfunding and determine whether 

this recommendation could be considered completed. However, after the time of the report, 

additional changes were made to the outage coordination methodology language that nullified 

the benefits of the new requirement. These changes including allowing for “late” planned 

outages that are not required to meet the 45-day deadline as previously approved by the 

ORWG. While these late outages will have a lower priority, the issue remains that there can be 

planned discrepancies between the outages in the transmission congestion rights models and 

the day-ahead market. As such, the MMU continues to recommend enhancements to address 

1662020 SPP Annual State of the Market Report, Chapter 8, 2020.1 Update market outage requirements to 
improve funding for transmission congestion rights 
167 SIR77 - TCR Funding 

State of the Market 2024 

216 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Recommendations 

the misalignment of planned outages in the transmission congestion rights and day-ahead 

market models. The MMU considers this a high priority issue.  

2020.2  ENHANCE MARKET-TO-MARKET EFFICIENCIES THROUGH 

COLLABORATION WITH MISO 

In 2019 and 2020, the MMU worked with the MISO market monitor on a series of 

recommendations for the Joint Regional State Committee / Organization of MISO States Seams 

Liaison Committee. Based on the results of the joint study, the MMU recommended in the 2020 

annual report to evaluate the processes and mechanisms used to effectuate the market-to-
market agreement between SPP and MISO.168 Several items identified by the market monitors 

have been addressed by SPP and MISO including activation of MISO market-to-market 

constraints in the SPP day-ahead market and increasing available relief of MISO resources. The 

MMU continues to recommend that SPP and its stakeholders address inefficiencies in the 

market-to-market agreement between SPP and MISO. The MMU considers this a medium 

priority issue. 

2020.3  RAISE OFFER FLOOR TO −$100/MWH 

In the 2020 report, the MMU recommended raising the energy offer floor to −$100/MWh. This 

recommendation was the result of analysis performed by the MMU which observed resources 

offering at the current offer floor of −$500/MWh and setting price. These offers did not 

represent cost and were harmful to nearby resources. As noted in the 2020 report, the MMU 

believes that raising the offer floor is a simple and cost-effective solution that avoids any 
limitation of what costs can be included in a market offer. This initiative169 is on the SPP 

Roadmap and is currently ranked as a low priority. The MMU considers this a low priority issue. 

2019.1  IMPROVE PRICE FORMATION  

In the 2019 report, the MMU identified circumstances where market prices provided neither 

short-term nor a long-term economic incentive to ensure reliability and recommended SPP and 

stakeholders review price formation during scarcity events and establish graduated demand 

curves that incentivize proper price formation.  

In the MMU report on the February 2021 winter weather event, the MMU made 

recommendations related to improving price formation during emergency and scarcity 

conditions. In that report, the MMU highlighted situations where price signals did not accurately 

168 2020 SPP Annual State of the Market Report, Chapter 8, 2020.2 Enhance market-to-market efficiencies 
through collaboration with MISO 
169 SIR76 - Mitigation of Unduly Low Offers 

State of the Market 2024 

217 

 
 
 
Southwest Power Pool, Inc. 
Market Monitoring Unit 

Recommendations 

reflect underlying conditions. These recommendations, which were aimed at improving pricing 

outcomes, closely aligned with the 2019 recommendation to improve price formation.  

In 2024, SPP proposed a revision to attachment AE of the tariff to address price formation 
during load shed events and emergency imports.170 This proposal was filed with FERC, accepted 
by FERC, and implemented by SPP on April 15, 2025.171 SPP is currently developing the next step 

to price formation which, if approved and accepted, will attempt to address price formation 

during emergency and near emergency events when behind-the-meter demand response 

resources are called on to reduce load. 

Both of these revisions are reasonable efforts to help ensure proper price formation during very 

specific events. While the MMU supports these efforts, the MMU continues to recommend that 

enhancements to improve price formation during emergency and near emergency events 

continue to include addressing increased resource commitments at the beginning of 

conservative operations. The additional capacity brought on-line in the form of increased 

headroom and early commitments in preparation for emergency conditions represents an 

excess of capacity that degrades market efficiency in the way of depressed market prices and 

increased uplift. Additionally, MISO’s updated operating reserve demand curve based on an 

increased value of lost load will create a strong disincentive for economic imports in the SPP 

region during emergency and near emergency events without improved price formation in the 

SPP market. The MMU considers this a medium priority issue. 

2019.2  INCENTIVIZE CAPACITY PERFORMANCE 

In the 2019 report, the MMU recommended that SPP and its stakeholders work to incentivize 

capacity performance and suggested potential options. In 2021, as part of its February 2021 

winter weather event report, the MMU also made recommendations regarding capacity 

adequacy and performance, many of which align with this recommendation. Specifically, the 

MMU noted there should be meaningful incentives for availability. Many of the MMU concerns 

have been noted in its comments in the filings for Performance-Based Accreditation (PBA) and 
Effective Load Carrying Capability (ELCC) accreditation for wind, solar and storage172 and the 
PBA based fuel assurance adder.173 The MMU considers this a high priority issue.

2019.3  UPDATE AND IMPROVE OUTAGE COORDINATION 

METHODOLOGY 

The MMU continues to recommend enhancements to SPP’s outage coordination methodology 

and to add language in its tariff that clearly describes the process and requirements. In the 2019 

annual report, the MMU initially recommended improvements. In 2022, the MMU highlighted its 
outstanding concerns regarding the outage coordination methodology.174 In addition, the MMU 
submitted initiatives in the SPP Roadmap process related to outage policy. 175  

SPP made some enhancements per the MMU recommendation and closed the initiative. SPP’s 
closure of the initiatives submitted by the MMU shows that they believe this issue is addressed. 

The MMU disagrees that its recommendations have been fully addressed and continues to 

recommend SPP make needed improvements to its outage coordination methodology, 

particularly in light of the central role outages play in the proposed accreditation methodologies 

previously noted. With the recommendation remaining incomplete, the MMU took the 

opportunity in the 2023 annual report to re-emphasize the importance of this recommendation 

and broaden its scope. In particular, the MMU recommends focusing on how the outage study 

and approval process interacts with all aspects of reliability, transmission hedging, and the 

market, including energy adequacy. The MMU continues to believe the current outage 

coordination methodology is not sufficient to ensure energy adequacy or reliability and needs 

to be, at least in part, in the tariff to ensure consistent application and enforceability. The MMU 

provided additional information and examples of why the current language is not sufficient. The 

MMU considers this a high priority issue. 

2018.1  LIMIT THE EXERCISE OF MARKET POWER  BY CREATING A 

BACKSTOP FOR PARAMETER CHANGES 

In its 2018 annual report, the MMU recommended that SPP strengthen the language regarding 

non-dollar-based parameters so that the expectation for the basis of these values is clear and 
well-defined and based on the actual capabilities of the resource.176 This recommendation was 
added as an initiative 177 on the SPP Roadmap in 2020 and is currently listed as medium priority.  

The MMU continues to highlight the need for this recommendation to be addressed due to 

continued concerns regarding non-dollar-based parameters being used to manipulate market

results and prices. As noted in the 2018 recommendation, the market is vulnerable to the 

exercise of market power by the manipulation of resources’ non-dollar-based parameters and, in 

addition, that non-dollar-based parameters should not be manipulated to affect market 

clearing.  

The MMU continues to recommend tariff changes to be made to address the potential to 

impact market results and prices through the manipulation of non-dollar-based resource 

parameters. The MMU recommends SPP include language in its tariff that requires information 

such as registration data, resource offers, and outage information to reasonably reflect the 

actual physical capabilities of the resources in the market. The MMU considers this a high 

priority issue. 

2018.2  ENHANCE CREDIT RULES TO ACCOUNT FOR KNOWN 

INFORMATION IN ASSESSMENTS 

In the 2018 report, the MMU made recommendations to improve SPP’s credit policy in light of a 

credit default in the PJM market that resulted in significant financial impacts to its market 
participants.178 The MMU engaged with SPP’s Credit Practice Working Group regarding this issue 

and the potential impact to SPP and its market participants. SPP stakeholders proposed a two-

phase approach to mitigate SPP’s exposure.  

SPP filed revisions to incorporate a mark-to-auction collateral requirement in August 2024.179 

The MMU filed comments originally supporting the SPP filing. After later identifying language in 

the filing that the MMU believed to be inconsistent with the FERC order, the MMU filed 

supplemental comments and limited protest of the filing. FERC accepted the SPP Tariff revisions 

in March 2025. However, FERC did not agree that the show cause requirement has been 

addressed. Additional discussions are ongoing to address FERC’s concerns. The MMU considers 

this a high priority issue. 

2017.2  ENHANCE COMMITMENT OF RESOURCES TO INCREASE 

RAMPING FLEXIBILITY 

In 2017, the MMU recommended that SPP and its stakeholders address the issue of inadequate 

ramping flexibility by modifying its market rules to enhance the commitment of resources and 

increase ramping flexibility. The MMU noted potential options to address the commitment 

concerns and recommended SPP and its stakeholders explore options, such as those noted, to

2017.3  ENHANCE MARKET RULES FOR ENERGY STORAGE 

RESOURCES 

SPP implemented its design for energy storage for compliance with FERC Order No. 841 in 2021. 

While the MMU filed supportive comments for the implementation of energy storage in the SPP 

market, the MMU noted further areas of enhancements to be considered with electric storage 

integration.  

Multiple initiatives were added to the SPP Roadmap to address the additional enhancements 

needed to fully integrate electric storage resources in the SPP markets. The MMU previously 

made comments regarding an SPP initiative that would make enhancements to the energy 

storage design. However, the MMU recommendation for inclusion of mitigation measures for 

excessively low offers was not reflected in the initiatives that were added to the SPP Roadmap. 

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and stakeholders consider the implementation of 

mitigation measures for excessively low offers in the market. The MMU considers this a medium 

priority issue. 

2017.4  ADDRESS INEFFICIENCY CAUSED BY SELF -COMMITTED 

RESOURCES 

The MMU recommended in the 2017 report that SPP and its stakeholders address the 

inefficiencies caused when market participants self-commit their resources in the market. The 

MMU then conducted an in-depth study of self-commitment practices and associated 
inefficiencies in 2019.180  

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and its stakeholders explore and develop ways to 

reduce instances of self-commitment of resources outside of the market solution, including 

considering adding an additional day to the optimization process. SPP submitted revision 
request 612 (multi-day economic commitment181) in January 2024. FERC rejected those revisions 

as filed. SPP is currently evaluating options for moving forward on a multi-day economic 

commitment design. The MMU considers this a low priority issue.

2017.5  ADDRESS INEFFICIENCY WHEN FORECASTED RESOURCES 

ARE UNDER-SCHEDULED DAY-AHEAD 

The MMU noted in its 2017 annual report that the systematic under-scheduling of wind 

resources in the day-ahead market can contribute to distorted price signals, suppressing real-
time prices and affecting revenue adequacy for all resources.182 The MMU has included this 

recommendation in each annual state of the market report since 2017 and included the 

recommendation in a special section of the 2022 annual report reemphasizing 
recommendations from previous reports that have not been addressed.183 Additional details 

regarding the recommendation can be found in those corresponding reports. 

The MMU raised this issue to the SPP Board of Directors in April 2023, noting its persistent 

impact on market efficiency. The Board directed stakeholders to develop a timeline to work on 

this initiative. SPP began working on this initiative, in collaboration with the MMU in early 2024. 

However, no significant progress has been made to address the noted concerns. The MMU 

considers this a high priority issue. 

2014.4  ADDRESS ISSUES WITH THE DAY -AHEAD MUST OFFER 

REQUIREMENT 

The MMU continues to recommend that SPP and stakeholders eliminate the limited day-ahead 

must-offer provision and revise the physical withholding rules to include a penalty for non-
compliance or address the design weaknesses. This initiative184 is on the SPP Roadmap and is 

currently ranked as a high priority, however no progress has been made. The MMU considers 

this a high priority issue.

July 2024. SPP submitted a filing in December 2024 setting the effective date of the Tariff 

revisions for January 2, 2025. While the revision request only addresses roughly half of the 

available congestion hedges in SPP, the MMU believes it is a step in the right direction to

2018.5  IMPROVE REGULATION MILEAGE PRICE FORMATION   

In the 2018 annual report, the MMU recommended SPP staff review the performance of 

regulation mileage and develop potential approaches to improve regulation mileage price 

formation. In addition, based on independent analysis, the MMU recommended SPP staff 

address two inefficiencies; one in the calculation of the factor used to determine average 
expected mileage, and one in the calculation of the clearing price for mileage.186 SPP 

implemented changes in June 2024 which address the MMU recommendations. The MMU 

considers this recommendation closed at this time.
